wer. Their cart was slower than Rowan's, but they had completed it together. Saff offered to compare wheels. The invitation was accepted immediately.","A local club representative liked their design. The prospective inventor did not have to decide his future that afternoon, Noor reminded Milo when he looked overwhelmed. He could take the leaflet home and ask his family. A welcome offer should not feel like an audition for a whole life. His shoulders relaxed.","The café had sold a dozen cakes and raised a million cents, its owner joked. Noor checked the more modest total. The scale's extra zero had been fixed, the old sign was out of date and replaced, and the video network's bandwidth finally sufficed. The fair had left small practical improvements all around the park.","Rowan collected the final cards. One illustrated a volt, another a watt, and the tractor card used horsepower. Their learning had been minimal in formal terms, yet the words now belonged to objects they remembered. The boy recalled a decimal error on the scale, a tenth marked at the wrong point. Saff remembered the litre jug because she had spilled it over Rowan's shoe.","\"A memorable kilo of water,\" he said.","\"A litre,\" she corrected.","\"I was testing you.\"","Her toothy grin suggested she believed no such thing. Noor laughed, and the sound made his failed joke more worthwhile than success. Irrespective of his reputation for charm, he was beginning to suspect that being laughed at by the right person could be better than admiration.","The children displayed a little drawing of the route: a kilometre around the park, a mile marker beyond it, and the peak behind the bandstand. They added the swath of flags, the ton of sand near the tractor, and the snack table. Milo could not conceive why the adults had left the snack table off the official map. A serious omission, Rowan agreed.","The kit's zip stuck as they packed. Rowan began to seethe, then realized the children were watching him with interest. He stopped, breathed, and asked for help. Saff freed the trapped thread. His good-natured thanks was not a performance; he was genuinely relieved. A tiny obstruction could perplex a grown man with excellent manners and far too many possessions.","The resting boy's parent checked the printed dosage on his medicine label, then closed the bag. Rowan waited until their family was ready before offering his chair. They did not need him to disrupt their routine. He would have made a lot of enthusiastic mistakes earlier; today he could stop at one quiet offer.","\"Do we get another patience ribbon?\" she asked Noor.","\"Perhaps a related certificate.\"","The family members arrived to collect their children. Rowan had staked out a place for the final photograph, but asked whether everyone wanted it before arranging them. Milo's mother did. Saff's father preferred a picture on his own phone. They took both, then let the children choose which cart details to show. Nobody needed to become promotional material simply because the day had been happy.","Afterwards, Rowan and Noor sat beneath a tree. He took on the job of dividing the last biscuits, a responsibility she trusted him to execute unevenly in her favor. The sugar might have offended a calorie chart. She ate it anyway. Their work was finished, and the afternoon's warmth made idleness unusually easy.","\"We built something,\" he said.","\"The children built something. We helped.\"","\"Ourselves included?\"","She looked at him for a moment. He was not intellectualizing the question. The warmth behind his teasing had become plain enough that she could answer without a joke.","\"Yes,\" she said.","His pleasure moved across his face slowly, then unstoppably. He did not turn the answer into a claim on her company. He offered another biscuit and let the small silence remain. Noor took it, their fingers touching briefly. Mara read the sign, looked at her husband, and asked whether he wanted his money back. Cassian's happily devoted expression became intense. \"Only if the answer is incorrect.\" The remainder of the evening was apparently ninety percent theory.","Their host, hotel entrepreneur Bellamy Voss, had dreamed up the Dance of Four Winds to enliven a stale resort season. The tourist stream had thinned, the vacancy rate had risen, and room turnover disappointed his investors. His answer combined entrepreneurship with matchmaking. A big-name guest who had studied at the University of Oxford would preside over the final score, supposedly measuring a couple's relational harmony.","\"An IQ test for love,\" Voss proclaimed.","\"A new paradigm for romance,\" the guest added.","June's baseline for the evening was free dinner. Mara's was fewer speeches. She could not see the relevance of IQ to a man remembering which tea his wife liked. Cassian agreed, though his eyes remained on a handsome dance instructor approaching her. \"You're jealous,\" she whispered.","It was evident that Voss wanted to highlight the invention, not the musicians. In contrast to Anton's easy humor, he seemed out of touch with the room. Mara took position beside Cassian, ready to go into the dance whenever the speeches ended. A bow from Anton could ignite trouble with her jealous husband, and she was inevitably tempted to tease.","\"Indicative of taste.\"","\"Not of causality,\" she replied. \"He has taught several people without marrying any of them.\"","The instructor's name was Anton. He bowed and offered guidance through the opening set. Its seventeenth step turned toward the southwest corner; its nineteenth required the partners to part briefly. Cassian considered that contrary to the spirit of the occasion. Anton laughed and explained the reciprocal figure. The woman would lead the next passage, contrary to the old norm.","The choreography looked progressive until Voss announced a percentage penalty for couples who did not match the required proportion of height. A paper described an inverse relationship between height difference and the incidence of errors. It ignored practice. The correlation was real within a tiny rehearsal group, but its relevance to tonight was untested. Mara would not let a decorative metric marginalize a shorter guest before she even moved.","\"A critical mass of nonsense,\" June said.","\"Don't persecute the adjectives,\" Mara told her. \"They have a difficult employer.\"","The guest appealed to proportionality. Mara asked whether he meant a mathematical ratio or a principle about fairness. His answer went into politics before returning to the dance. The distinction hit home with several listeners. They had not come to diagnose the host's intellectual habits, but neither did they need to lean on his confidence.","A concertgoer recognized Anton from a lively stage performance. He was delighted by the woman's praise, appropriately returning it to the musicians. Voss, in contrast, preferred the executive suite full of sponsors. His multinational partner wanted a report on attendance, not an evening about kinship. The latter theme had appeared in the program because it sounded warmer than room sales.","Mara would abide by ordinary courtesy, but the height rule frustrated her. A girl beside her had brought a tall partner and expected to be expelled from the competition. Voss reassured her with a theatrical exception. It was distasteful to turn an unnecessary rule into an occasion for generosity. Mara privately resolved to lead the charge against the scoring system with her feet.","The floor's grained wood had been divided by removable tape. Horizontal marks guided the opening formation; vertical lines indicated the next pair's place. Northeast and southeast signs stood beside the musicians. The southwest corner held a vase which had apparently survived since the nineteenth century. June suggested moving it before anybody demonstrated enthusiasm. A waiter did so, earning more gratitude than the scoring lecture.","Voss proudly announced that the majority of entrants approved the system. The index came from a questionnaire completed before the rule was explained. Mara asked how much time could elapse before they actually danced. The old-fashioned clock's hand seemed to stagnate. Voss said the question was irrelevant. His out-dated card specified five minutes for instructions; he had used twenty.","\"Get moving,\" Cassian murmured to her.","\"At last. An imperative I like.\"","He offered his hand. They would build their rhythm from the bottom up, starting with the feet before considering theory. Their first turn was lively enough to make June applaud. Mara seldom felt passive in his arms; his attention created room rather than instruction. A naughty little change of direction nearly made him miss the next beat.","\"Untested maneuver,\" he said.","\"Resilient partner.\"","A woman crossing behind them spilled a glass. The soluble dye in a cheap streamer ran across Mara's sleeve. The stain might have made another hostess flee. Mara laughed and rolled the wet fabric into a narrow cuff. Anton supplied a napkin while Cassian minimized the commotion by moving the fallen glass out of the route. The woman's apology met kindness, not a portion of borrowed grandeur.","The music stopped abruptly. Voss's machine showed a catastrophic compatibility score. Their improvised turn had broken its expected sequence. The incidence of mistakes supposedly predicted future unhappiness. In that sense, the system had just diagnosed a marriage it had never met.","Mara looked at Cassian. \"We should contrast the prediction with experience.\"","He drew her close. The kiss was brief and thoroughly public. June looked at the score and laughed. The evening's farcical promise had finally become useful entertainment."],"questions":[],"wordCount":921,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0259\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：The Dance of Four Winds</div><h1>The IQ of a Kiss</h1><p id=\"chapter-0259-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> ballroom <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6524\" data-words=\"6524\">promised</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8538\" data-words=\"8538\">to</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2467\" data-words=\"2467\">distill</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8693\" data-words=\"8693\">true</a> compatibility <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3450\" data-words=\"3450\">from</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5722\" data-words=\"5722\">one</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2102\" data-words=\"2102\">dance</a>. The causality in his model remained sound, he insisted, and its compatibility percentage should be treated as indicative. Mara would not let a contrary result become evidence that the model was right. She asked whether the dance had tested their marriage or only their ability to obey a sequence. Inevitably, a passive audience became restless. A laugh could ignite it faster than an argument.","\"Relational coordination,\" the Oxford guest said.","\"Then call it that.\"","The relevance of his metric had become painfully visible. An IQ score could not distill a person's kindness; a dance error could not establish the future. A small correlation in rehearsals might suggest a question, yet the model's claim went further. Cassian's intense dislike of being measured was scarcely a controlled experiment either. Mara took his hand before he could say something elegant and devastating.","\"Don't flee,\" she whispered. \"I'm enjoying the farce.\" June found the prolonged defense distasteful and let Anton lead the charge back toward music.","\"I'm happily at your disposal.\"","The instructor proposed a contrast. Let the musicians choose the tempo, let the dancers practise, and count which errors diminished. The idea would go into a revised second round. Its norm would be learning, not instant ranking. The host looked as if his paradigm had been insulted. June suggested he could dream up another slogan while everyone else moved.","The floor needed attention first. The soluble dye had stained a patch of tape, and a little water remained on the grained boards. Anton called a pause, then helped the staff dry it. A resilient assistant replaced the marker before it could become slippery. The small task was appropriately shared. Nobody's claim to expertise made the wet floor safe by itself.","The University of Oxford guest finally introduced himself as a historian of commerce, not a specialist in relationships. His big-name institution had been given a role he had not checked closely enough. A multinational sponsor wanted an authoritative face, and he had enjoyed being asked. The admission made him more approachable than his earlier lecture had done.","The majority of the guests wanted the stale lecture finished. An incorrect announcement had given them contradictory directions, and the farcical result was a circle of people facing away from their partners. Anton asked them to get moving clockwise. His humor did not expel anyone for being slow. Mara's delighted face made it evident that this would be her highlight.","Mara liked the honesty. He still wanted to defend the game's entrepreneurship: the hotel employed local people, and its slump affected them. But room turnover and the vacancy rate did not decide whether a scoring rule was fair. The tourist stream could be encouraged by pleasure rather than a promise of scientific certainty. Voss's own liveliness, once freed from a script, was unexpectedly funny.","He told a story about his seventeenth birthday. A date had rejected him after the nineteenth attempt to teach him a step. \"Possibly an inverse relationship between effort and charm,\" he said.","\"A case with no control group,\" June replied.","The laughter hit home without humiliating him. Voss could have remained out of touch, defending every adjective. Instead, he handed Anton the microphone. A managerial retreat did not have to be a personal catastrophe. His formal title seldom helped him keep a beat anyway.","The new opening began in the southwest. Northeast pairs crossed horizontal lines, while southeast pairs moved along vertical ones. The drawings looked difficult until Anton taught them from the bottom up. The proportion of successful crossings rose. His reminder about the ratio of steps to beats was practical, not an oracle. The percentage on the screen now described what had actually happened.","The incidence of collisions fell when the dancers had room. An awkward partnership was not always an interpersonal failure; a narrow corner could frustrate anyone. The question of proportionality had acquired a real use in arranging space. The old vase stayed safely on a shelf. June called its survival a triumph for progressive politics and common sense, which made the historian choke on his drink.","Mara had no desire to persecute Voss. The contradiction between his claims and the outcome was enough. A critical mass of guests wanted to dance, and the remainder wanted to watch. Nobody needed an out-dated penalty to keep them interested. A host could lean on the musicians for the evening without pretending his machine understood every heart in the room.","A jealous young man refused to let his partner dance with Anton. She reminded him that the rotating figure had been explained. Mara glanced at Cassian, who lifted both hands in theatrical innocence. His earlier expression was evidently not forgotten. He would not marginalize another guest to minimize a harmless moment of discomfort.","\"Untested theory,\" she told him.","\"You'll find me a willing student.\"","They took position beside the next pair. The latter couple were celebrating forty years together and laughed at every missed step. Their kinship seemed rooted in reciprocal amusement. The older man was a concertgoer who cared about music more than his feet. He was an excellent partner because he kept making room for his wife.","The naughty reversal came again. Mara led Cassian toward the window, then turned him back into the circle. It was an imperative delivered through movement, one he followed proudly. The intensity of his attention left her breathless. In contrast to a score, it had no useful decimal place.","A few minutes elapsed before either noticed the applause. The executive suite had emptied; sponsors had joined the crowd. In that sense, the host's plan had succeeded after losing its original claim. Voss declined to measure the moment. He poured water instead, and the lively evening continued."],"questions":[],"wordCount":962,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0260\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：The Voss presented it without invoking IQ, causality, or a hidden law of attraction. The percentage improvement was a modest metric, not a judgment on their love. The couple were delighted by the distinction: they won a dinner, and nobody told them whom to marry. Ten percent of the audience cheered before the name was announced. No theory whatsoever was required.","Mara stood beside Cassian, watching the host distill his farcical presentation into three cheerful sentences. The stale language about perfect matches had gone. He would not pretend the remaining game could prove a relational truth. A contrast between first and later attempts was enough to explain the prize.","The historian wrote a short note about the hotel venture. Its entrepreneurship had brought an unusual evening to a quiet town. The tourist stream, vacancy rate, and room turnover would require longer records. A lively ballroom was encouraging, but one night would not establish a trend. Voss accepted that without looking diminished. His pride had found a more durable place in being a good host.","Cassian asked Mara whether she wanted to leave. She looked toward the dancers, then the open garden. \"A moment outside.\"","The nineteenth turn was the highlight for Cassian: Mara ended it against his chest. The coincidence did not proclaim a correlation between counting and happiness. Her smile was indicative of amusement, and he would happily let several minutes elapse before moving. A remnant of blue tape clung to her heel. He removed it with a bow, inevitably making her laugh.","He took her hand. A jealous husband might have hated the evening; this one had learned to laugh at his own face. The untested prediction of incompatibility had become a joke they could keep. She did not need to persecute the host for giving it to them.","The garden path led southwest beneath a row of lamps. A nineteenth-century bench waited beside the wall, polished by years of visitors. Mara's seventeenth step took her beyond its damp patch and into Cassian's arms. \"Excellent counting,\" he said. It took very little to ignite her wish to kiss him again.","\"I have a wide range of accomplishments.\"","\"An inverse relationship to your modesty.\"","She laughed and leaned on him. In contrast to the bright hall, the garden held a quiet rhythm of wind and distant strings. Her stained sleeve had dried into a pattern of pale blue. Cassian touched the improvised cuff. \"I liked your solution.\"","\"I liked your distraction.\"","\"The kiss was a research method.\"","\"A tenuous claim of causality.\"","He kissed her again, and she decided the replication was every bit as pleasant as the first result. The relevance of anyone else's judgment receded. The incidence of spontaneous laughter remained high. No one was counting now, which left them free to enjoy it.","Inside, Anton taught another group. His instructions began from the bottom up, giving people a rhythm before complicated turns. The proportion of passive spectators diminished as they grew confident enough to join. The old norm of immediate elegance had been replaced by permission to learn. That was a paradigm Mara could support without signing a questionnaire.","A couple came out to ask for a photograph. They had admired the duchess's refusal to flee after the spill, but she made them laugh by admitting that she had not known what else to do. Her composure was seldom a rehearsed virtue. Sometimes it was simply a contrary impulse to enjoy what someone expected would embarrass her.","They went back through the doors together. The host proudly directed a waiter to bring fresh drinks. His earlier insistence on proportionality now concerned the number of cups and guests. An appropriate use, June declared. Voss bowed to the correction. She could criticize a claim without trying to expel the person who made it from society.","The remaining theory had become simple. A guest could choose to dance, watch, or go into the garden. The host did not marginalize anyone for declining. His compatibility percentage no longer appeared over their heads. It was evident that people were more at ease. In that sense, the removal of an oracle had improved the party.","Cassian watched Anton ask Mara for the last dance. The invitation was public and courteous. For a moment, his expression became intense again. Mara gave him a naughty smile, then declined the instructor. She wanted the final dance with her husband. It was a choice, not an obligation he had won by glaring.","They took position under the lights. Her imperative was a soft squeeze of his fingers. He followed the first turn without trying to minimize the distance she wanted to cover. The music was happily beyond the scorecard now. His hand remained steady at her back.","A young guest asked Voss how he would advertise next year. \"I may dream up a less scientific title,\" he said.","\"Or get moving and learn to dance,\" June suggested.","His laugh hit home in the most useful way. He joined the line, looking briefly out of touch with his own feet, then found the beat. The show was no longer an untested promise to diagnose strangers. It was a room full of people making mistakes together.","Mara let Cassian turn her once more. Their laughter went with them across the floor, warm and quite impossible to distill into a number."],"questions":[],"wordCount":908,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0261\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：The Dance of Four Winds</div><h1>The Last Dance Is Hers</h1><p id=\"chapter-0261-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3238\" data-words=\"3238\">final</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6470\" data-words=\"6470\">prize</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3623\" data-words=\"3623\">went</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8538\" data-words=\"8538\">to</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1959\" data-words=\"1959\">couple</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9216\" data-words=\"9216\">who</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3834\" data-words=\"3834\">had</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2102\" data-words=\"2102\">danced</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9307\" data-words=\"9307\">worst</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-593\" data-words=\"593\">at</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-898\" data-words=\"898,896\">beginning</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-383\" data-words=\"383\">and</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4777\" data-words=\"4777,4774\">learned</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5350\" data-words=\"5350\">most</a>. class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"259\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"261\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x056c01","title":"The Woman in the Arrival Coat","volume":"婚后外传：The Star He Could Not Keep","anchor":"chapter-0262","paragraphs":["The fashion mogul looked at Mara's plain arrival coat and asked whether she had come to repair the furniture. Cassian's smile became diplomatic. His wife was less restrained. \"Only if it has better manners.\" Her unthinkingly direct reply made Della grin.","Della Mire nearly dropped her saucer. She had invited them to the gala because Mara had once restored a set of buttons for her. Della was job-hunting after an ill-managed workshop closed. Her hard science degree had led her into textile design, where she was persistently told that engineering lacked elegance. Her distinctive clay brooches were the answer: she could bake something beautiful without pretending a crystal changed its owner's fate.","The mogul, Albin Rusk, adored astrological motifs. His art-loving guests wore moons, planets, and one alarming pyramid. He had asked Della to make an oval star brooch, then described it as a quaint apprentice piece in his magazine. The damning sentence had followed a photograph cropped so severely that half her design disappeared. His fashion image depended on making useful people seem replaceable.","Tonight he proposed a wager. The brooch would be hidden somewhere in the gallery. If Della could find it before the final bell, he would pay for twenty pieces at her quoted price. If she failed, he wanted the original at no charge. Della had refused, but his assistant publicly announced her acceptance. She stood on the verge of leaving when Mara offered to take her place.","\"You may be backed by a duke,\" Rusk said, \"but this is not your little workshop.\"","Della could not afford to mark time. In desperation, she had brought copious sketches to every possible buyer. In the case of this brooch, she had stated the price clearly. Rusk's wager could not exempt him from paying. Mara would attempt to find it, working collaboratively with its maker. She had no wish to get out of a contest that might drive her point home so beautifully.","\"Then leave the finding to me.\"","The mansion was home to an extravagant collection. An oval mirror faced a cylinder of colored glass. A bronze pyramid stood on a low base, and a zigzag path of lamps led into the garden. Contextual clues, Rusk promised, would confer an advantage on the observant. His deep-rooted wish to be admired made him assume nobody else would notice the signs first.","Della studied the room relationally, comparing shapes rather than prices. She was skilled at seeing how a curve answered an angle. Her evolutionary research into natural fibers had taught her to look for structure, but this was no hard science test. Rusk had selectively chosen his own favorite pieces. The challenge reflected his taste, not an inexorable law. The host tried to beef up his instructions while the others waited.","Mara made a quick jog around the room before the formal start. Cassian offered to distract the host. \"A fruitful use of your charms,\" she said.","\"An expense I can afford.\"","A painted map showed ships arriving in the New World. Beneath it lay an approximate model of the green land beyond an old port. Della thought the gallery's fixed route a rut. She could work flexibly instead. A biodegradable paper sleeve protected the clues; the extravagant packaging seemed distinctly odd. Her miserable morning would improve if she refused to worry about the host's approval.","\"Don't expend them all.\"","He grinned, the first wrinkle beside his eye deepening. Della's misery began to lift. If someone could navigate this peculiar battlefield, she suspected it was the woman whose husband looked at her as if she were a private sunrise.","Rusk ordered the staff to clamp down on unauthorized movement. Mara asked what the wager allowed. His assistant produced a concise card. The search included the main gallery and open garden, with no private rooms. Staff could respond to questions. Nobody would force a door or damage an exhibit. Mara would gladly take responsibility for her own conduct; she would not accept rules changed after the start. Cassian brought the little crowd under control before its out-of-control enthusiasm made matters worse. Della had always aspired to make beautiful things.","The first clue concerned a triangle and a dot. Della took a handful of printed images and arranged them on the table. She could crop a border from a photograph, but she could not recover what the magazine had removed from its reader's view. The original image revealed a small star behind the brooch. Its light pointed toward the garden.","The theory was solvable, and the route looked accessible. Rusk's increasing impatience did not appal Mara. She had met big men in worse tempers. She asked him to stand down from the doorway he was blocking. His laugh intensified into a challenge. \"Prove yourself, then.\"","She bowed and walked past him. The out-of-town guests followed at a distance. A profit-making spectacle was supposed to elevate his reputation; he had not considered that an opponent might enjoy it. Mara could envisage a much better ending for Della. A small toxic rivalry would not decide the designer's entire living standard.","In the garden, a wedge of moonlight fell across the rim of a pool. The water had a shallow dip beside a coarse stone cube. An astrological wheel rotated above it, driven by a simple mechanism. Della recognized the cause and effect: its weight moved a linked pointer, selecting an opening in the outer ring. The apparatus had hitherto been presented as pure mystery.","A gust caught the wheel's decorative fringe. Its ribbons bound around the axle, and the display lurched. The pointer struck a lamp, which rolled toward the pool. Cassian caught it before it fell. The mechanism was not electrical at that point, but a falling glass shade could hurt someone. Della asked the technician to stop it before the wheel went out of control.","The host laughed as if danger enhanced the party. Mara did not. She had not come to go to war with a peacock; she had come to win a brooch.
